# Copyright 2021, New York University and the TUF contributors
# SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT OR Apache-2.0

"""Test utility to simulate a repository

RepositorySimulator provides methods to modify repository metadata so that it's
easy to "publish" new repository versions with modified metadata, while serving
the versions to client test code.

RepositorySimulator implements FetcherInterface so Updaters in tests can use it
as a way to "download" new metadata from remote: in practice no downloading,
network connections or even file access happens as RepositorySimulator serves
everything from memory.

Metadata and targets "hosted" by the simulator are made available in URL paths
"/metadata/..." and "/targets/..." respectively.

Example::

    # constructor creates repository with top-level metadata
    sim = RepositorySimulator()

    # metadata can be modified directly: it is immediately available to clients
    sim.snapshot.version += 1

    # As an exception, new root versions require explicit publishing
    sim.root.version += 1
    sim.publish_root()

    # there are helper functions
    sim.add_target("targets", b"content", "targetpath")
    sim.targets.version += 1
    sim.update_snapshot()

    # Use the simulated repository from an Updater:
    updater = Updater(
        dir,
        "https://example.com/metadata/",
        dir,
        "https://example.com/targets/",
        sim,
        bootstrap=sim.signed_roots[0],
    )
    updater.refresh()
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import logging
import os
import tempfile
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
from urllib import parse

from securesystemslib.signer import CryptoSigner, Signer

from tuf.api.exceptions import DownloadHTTPError
from tuf.api.metadata import (
    SPECIFICATION_VERSION,
    TOP_LEVEL_ROLE_NAMES,
    DelegatedRole,
    Delegations,
    Metadata,
    MetaFile,
    Root,
    Snapshot,
    SuccinctRoles,
    TargetFile,
    Targets,
    Timestamp,
)
from tuf.api.serialization.json import JSONSerializer
from tuf.ngclient.fetcher import FetcherInterface

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from collections.abc import Iterator

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

SPEC_VER = ".".join(SPECIFICATION_VERSION)

_HASH_ALGORITHM = "sha256"


@dataclass
class FetchTracker:
    """Fetcher counter for metadata and targets."""

    metadata: list[tuple[str, int | None]] = field(default_factory=list)
    targets: list[tuple[str, str | None]] = field(default_factory=list)


@dataclass
class RepositoryTarget:
    """Contains actual target data and the related target metadata."""

    data: bytes
    target_file: TargetFile


class RepositorySimulator(FetcherInterface):
    """Simulates a repository that can be used for testing."""

    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.md_delegates: dict[str, Metadata[Targets]] = {}

        # other metadata is signed on-demand (when fetched) but roots must be
        # explicitly published with publish_root() which maintains this list
        self.signed_roots: list[bytes] = []

        # signers are used on-demand at fetch time to sign metadata
        # keys are roles, values are dicts of {keyid: signer}
        self.signers: dict[str, dict[str, Signer]] = {}

        # target downloads are served from this dict
        self.target_files: dict[str, RepositoryTarget] = {}

        # Whether to compute hashes and length for meta in snapshot/timestamp
        self.compute_metafile_hashes_length = False

        # Enable hash-prefixed target file names
        self.prefix_targets_with_hash = True

        self.dump_dir: str | None = None
        self.dump_version = 0

        self.fetch_tracker = FetchTracker()

        now = datetime.now(UTC)
        self.safe_expiry = now.replace(microsecond=0) + timedelta(days=30)

        self._initialize()

    @property
    def root(self) -> Root:
        return self.md_root.signed

    @property
    def timestamp(self) -> Timestamp:
        return self.md_timestamp.signed

    @property
    def snapshot(self) -> Snapshot:
        return self.md_snapshot.signed

    @property
    def targets(self) -> Targets:
        return self.md_targets.signed

    def all_targets(self) -> Iterator[tuple[str, Targets]]:
        """Yield role name and signed portion of targets one by one."""
        yield Targets.type, self.md_targets.signed
        for role, md in self.md_delegates.items():
            yield role, md.signed

    def add_signer(self, role: str, signer: Signer) -> None:
        if role not in self.signers:
            self.signers[role] = {}
        self.signers[role][signer.public_key.keyid] = signer

    def rotate_keys(self, role: str) -> None:
        """remove all keys for role, then add threshold of new keys"""
        self.root.roles[role].keyids.clear()
        self.signers[role].clear()
        for _ in range(self.root.roles[role].threshold):
            signer = CryptoSigner.generate_ed25519()
            self.root.add_key(signer.public_key, role)
            self.add_signer(role, signer)

    def _initialize(self) -> None:
        """Setup a minimal valid repository."""

        self.md_targets = Metadata(Targets(expires=self.safe_expiry))
        self.md_snapshot = Metadata(Snapshot(expires=self.safe_expiry))
        self.md_timestamp = Metadata(Timestamp(expires=self.safe_expiry))
        self.md_root = Metadata(Root(expires=self.safe_expiry))

        for role in TOP_LEVEL_ROLE_NAMES:
            signer = CryptoSigner.generate_ed25519()
            self.md_root.signed.add_key(signer.public_key, role)
            self.add_signer(role, signer)

        self.publish_root()

    def publish_root(self) -> None:
        """Sign and store a new serialized version of root."""
        self.md_root.signatures.clear()
        for signer in self.signers[Root.type].values():
            self.md_root.sign(signer, append=True)

        self.signed_roots.append(self.md_root.to_bytes(JSONSerializer()))
        logger.debug("Published root v%d", self.root.version)

    def _fetch(self, url: str) -> Iterator[bytes]:
        """Fetches data from the given url and returns an Iterator (or yields
        bytes).
        """
        path = parse.urlparse(url).path
        if path.startswith("/metadata/") and path.endswith(".json"):
            # figure out rolename and version
            ver_and_name = path[len("/metadata/") :][: -len(".json")]
            version_str, _, role = ver_and_name.partition(".")
            # root is always version-prefixed while timestamp is always NOT
            if role == Root.type or (
                self.root.consistent_snapshot and ver_and_name != Timestamp.type
            ):
                version: int | None = int(version_str)
        self.snapshot.version += 1
        self.update_timestamp()

    def _get_delegator(self, delegator_name: str) -> Targets:
        """Given a delegator name return, its corresponding Targets object."""
        if delegator_name == Targets.type:
            return self.targets

        return self.md_delegates[delegator_name].signed

    def add_target(self, role: str, data: bytes, path: str) -> None:
        """Create a target from data and add it to the target_files."""
        targets = self._get_delegator(role)

        target = TargetFile.from_data(path, data, ["sha256"])
        targets.targets[path] = target
        self.target_files[path] = RepositoryTarget(data, target)

    def add_delegation(
        self, delegator_name: str, role: DelegatedRole, targets: Targets
    ) -> None:
        """Add delegated target role to the repository."""
        delegator = self._get_delegator(delegator_name)

        if (
            delegator.delegations is not None
            and delegator.delegations.succinct_roles is not None
        ):
            raise ValueError("Can't add a role when succinct_roles is used")

        # Create delegation
        if delegator.delegations is None:
            delegator.delegations = Delegations({}, roles={})

        assert delegator.delegations.roles is not None
        # put delegation last by default
        delegator.delegations.roles[role.name] = role

        # By default add one new key for the role
        signer = CryptoSigner.generate_ed25519()
        delegator.add_key(signer.public_key, role.name)
        self.add_signer(role.name, signer)

        # Add metadata for the role
        if role.name not in self.md_delegates:
            self.md_delegates[role.name] = Metadata(targets, {})

    def add_succinct_roles(
        self, delegator_name: str, bit_length: int, name_prefix: str
    ) -> None:
        """Add succinct roles info to a delegator with name "delegator_name".

        Note that for each delegated role represented by succinct roles an empty
        Targets instance is created.
        """
        delegator = self._get_delegator(delegator_name)

        if (
            delegator.delegations is not None
            and delegator.delegations.roles is not None
        ):
            raise ValueError(
                "Can't add a succinct_roles when delegated roles are used"
            )

        signer = CryptoSigner.generate_ed25519()
        succinct_roles = SuccinctRoles([], 1, bit_length, name_prefix)
        delegator.delegations = Delegations({}, None, succinct_roles)

        # Add targets metadata for all bins.
        for delegated_name in succinct_roles.get_roles():
            self.md_delegates[delegated_name] = Metadata(
                Targets(expires=self.safe_expiry)
            )

            self.add_signer(delegated_name, signer)

        delegator.add_key(signer.public_key)

    def write(self) -> None:
        """Dump current repository metadata to self.dump_dir

        This is a debugging tool: dumping repository state before running
        Updater refresh may be useful while debugging a test.
        """
        if self.dump_dir is None:
            self.dump_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp()
            print(f"Repository Simulator dumps in {self.dump_dir}")

        self.dump_version += 1
        dest_dir = os.path.join(self.dump_dir, str(self.dump_version))
        os.makedirs(dest_dir)

        for ver in range(1, len(self.signed_roots) + 1):
            with open(os.path.join(dest_dir, f"{ver}.root.json"), "wb") as f:
                f.write(self.fetch_metadata(Root.type, ver))

        for role in [Timestamp.type, Snapshot.type, Targets.type]:
            with open(os.path.join(dest_dir, f"{role}.json"), "wb") as f:
                f.write(self.fetch_metadata(role))

    def __len__(self) -> int:
        """Return number of ``Signed`` objects in ``TrustedMetadataSet``."""
        return len(self._trusted_set)

    def iter_targets(self) -> Iterator[tuple[str, str, TargetFile]]:
        """Yield ``(role, path, target)`` for trusted targets metadata.

        Iteration follows the order in which roles were added to the trusted
        set. Only data that has already passed TUF verification is returned;
        this method never performs network or filesystem I/O.
        """
        for role, signed in self._trusted_set.items():
            if isinstance(signed, Targets):
                for path, target in signed.targets.items():
                    yield role, path, target

    def iter_roles(self) -> Iterator[tuple[str, Signed]]:
        """Yield ``(role, signed)`` pairs in trusted-set insertion order."""
        yield from self._trusted_set.items()

    def __iter__(self) -> Iterator[Signed]:
        """Return iterator over ``Signed`` objects in
        ``TrustedMetadataSet``.
        """
        return iter(self._trusted_set.values())

    # Helper properties for top level metadata
    @property
    def root(self) -> Root:
        )

# Copyright 2026, the TUF contributors

"""Tests for the offline target combination preflight."""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
import tempfile
import unittest

from tests.repository_simulator import RepositorySimulator
from tuf.api.metadata import TargetFile, Targets
from tuf.ngclient import Updater
from tuf.ngclient.combination import (
    CombinationConflictError,
    TargetCandidate,
    preflight_combination,
)


def make_target(
    path: str,
    module: str | None = None,
    version: str | None = None,
    provides: dict[str, str] | None = None,
    requires: object = None,
    config: str | None = None,
    content: bytes | None = None,
) -> TargetFile:
    data = content if content is not None else f"{path}:{module}".encode()
    target = TargetFile.from_data(path, data, ["sha256"])
    custom: dict[str, object] = {}
    if module is not None:
        custom["module"] = module
    if version is not None:
        custom["version"] = version
    if provides is not None:
        custom["provides"] = provides
    if requires is not None:
        custom["requires"] = requires
    if config is not None:
        custom["config"] = config
    if custom:
        target.unrecognized_fields["custom"] = custom
    return target


def make_view(*targets: TargetFile) -> dict[str, Targets]:
    return {
        Targets.type: Targets(
            targets={target.path: target for target in targets}
        )
    }


class TestCombinationPreflight(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_transitive_closure_and_evidence(self) -> None:
        app = make_target(
            "modules/app",
            "app",
            "1.0",
            {"app-api": "1.0.0"},
            [{"module": "lib", "interface": "lib-api", "version": ">=2,<3"}],
        )
        lib = make_target(
            "modules/lib",
            "lib",
            "2.1.0",
            {"lib-api": "2.4.0"},
            ["util"],
        )
        util = make_target("modules/util", "util", "1.0.0")

        plan = preflight_combination(
            make_view(app, lib, util),
            ["modules/app"],
            {"app-api": "1.0.0", "lib-api": ">=2"},
        )

        self.assertTrue(plan)
        self.assertEqual(set(plan.targets), {"app", "lib", "util"})
        self.assertIs(plan["app"], app)
        self.assertIs(plan.get("modules/lib"), lib)
        self.assertEqual(plan.get("nope"), None)
        self.assertEqual(
            [(edge.module, edge.dependency) for edge in plan.dependencies],
            [("app", "lib"), ("lib", "util")],
        )
        app_lib = plan.dependencies[0]
        self.assertEqual(app_lib.provided_version, "2.4.0")
        self.assertTrue(app_lib.satisfied)
        self.assertEqual(plan.targets_by_path["modules/app"], app)
        self.assertEqual(dict(plan.caller_interfaces)["app-api"], "1.0.0")

    def test_candidate_wrapper_and_channel_mapping(self) -> None:
        app = make_target("app", "app", requires=["lib"])
        lib = make_target("lib", "lib")

        plan = preflight_combination(
            make_view(app, lib),
            {"stable": [TargetCandidate(app, channel="stable")]},
        )
        self.assertTrue(plan.ok)
        self.assertEqual(set(plan.targets), {"app", "lib"})
        self.assertEqual(plan.dependencies[0].channel, "stable")

    def test_cycle_conflict_and_allow_cycles(self) -> None:
        a = make_target("a", "a", requires=["b"])
        b = make_target("b", "b", requires=["a"])
        view = make_view(a, b)

        plan = preflight_combination(view, ["a"])
        self.assertFalse(plan.ok)
        assert plan.conflict is not None
        self.assertEqual(plan.conflict.kind, "cycle")
        self.assertEqual(plan.conflict.cycle, ("a", "b", "a"))
        self.assertTrue(plan.conflict.is_cycle)
        self.assertEqual(plan.targets, {})
        self.assertEqual(

===OUTPUT call_00_u68pdmzg3krwlf5v2smk7tmk ===
Exit code: 0

        self.assertEqual(
            plan.first_unsatisfiable, ("a -> b", "b -> a")
        )

        allowed = preflight_combination(view, ["a"], allow_cycles=True)
        self.assertTrue(allowed.ok)
        self.assertEqual(set(allowed.targets), {"a", "b"})

    def test_conflicting_identity_across_channels(self) -> None:
        stable = make_target(
            "stable/plugin", "plugin", "1.0", content=b"stable content"
        )
        beta = make_target(
            "beta/plugin", "plugin", "1.0", content=b"beta content"
        )

        plan = preflight_combination(
            make_view(stable, beta),
            ["stable/plugin", "beta/plugin"],
        )

        self.assertFalse(plan.ok)
        assert plan.conflict is not None
        self.assertEqual(plan.conflict.kind, "identity")
        self.assertTrue(plan.conflict.is_identity_conflict)
        self.assertEqual(set(plan.conflict.channels), {"targets"})
        self.assertEqual(len(plan.conflict.identities), 2)
        self.assertIn("plugin", plan.conflict.modules)
        self.assertEqual(set(plan.attempted_targets), {"plugin"})

    def test_config_mismatch_only_when_combined(self) -> None:
        a = make_target("a", "a", config="cfg/v1")
        b = make_target("b", "b", config="cfg/v2")
        view = make_view(a, b)

        first = preflight_combination(view, ["a"])
        second = preflight_combination(view, ["b"])
        self.assertTrue(first.ok)
        self.assertTrue(second.ok)

        combined = preflight_combination(view, ["a", "b"])
        self.assertFalse(combined.ok)
        assert combined.conflict is not None
        self.assertEqual(combined.conflict.reason, "config-mismatch")
        self.assertEqual(combined.conflict.kind, "unsatisfiable")
        self.assertEqual(len(combined.conflict.group), 2)

    def test_interface_constraint_group(self) -> None:
        lib = make_target("lib", "lib", provides={"lib-api": "2.4.0"})
        first = make_target(
            "first",
            "first",
            requires=[
                {"module": "lib", "interface": "lib-api", "version": ">=2,<3"}
            ],
        )
        second = make_target(
            "second",
            "second",
            requires=[
                {"module": "lib", "interface": "lib-api", "version": "<2"}
            ],
        )

        plan = preflight_combination(make_view(lib, first, second), ["first", "second"])

        self.assertFalse(plan.ok)
        assert plan.conflict is not None
        self.assertEqual(plan.conflict.kind, "unsatisfiable")
        self.assertEqual(plan.conflict.reason, "interface-version")
        self.assertEqual(len(plan.conflict.group), 2)
        self.assertTrue(
            any("first" in item for item in plan.conflict.group)
        )
        self.assertTrue(
            any("second" in item for item in plan.conflict.group)
        )

    def test_missing_dependency(self) -> None:
        app = make_target("app", "app", requires=["not-present"])
        plan = preflight_combination(make_view(app), ["app"])

        self.assertFalse(plan.ok)
        assert plan.conflict is not None
        self.assertEqual(plan.conflict.kind, "missing")
        self.assertEqual(plan.conflict.reason, "missing-dependency")
        self.assertIn("not-present", plan.conflict.modules)

    def test_missing_candidate_is_reported(self) -> None:
        plan = preflight_combination(make_view(), ["not/in/view"])
        self.assertFalse(plan.ok)
        assert plan.conflict is not None
        self.assertEqual(plan.conflict.kind, "missing")
        self.assertEqual(plan.conflict.reason, "missing-candidate")

    def test_raise_on_conflict(self) -> None:
        app = make_target("app", "app", requires=["missing"])
        with self.assertRaises(CombinationConflictError) as raised:
            preflight_combination(
                make_view(app), ["app"], raise_on_conflict=True
            )
        self.assertEqual(raised.exception.conflict.kind, "missing")
        self.assertEqual(raised.exception.kind, "missing")


class TestCombinationOfflineMirrors(unittest.TestCase):
    def setUp(self) -> None:
        self.temp_dirs: list[tempfile.TemporaryDirectory[str]] = []

    def tearDown(self) -> None:
        for temp_dir in self.temp_dirs:
            temp_dir.cleanup()

    def _mirror_updater(self) -> tuple[Updater, RepositorySimulator]:
        temp_dir = tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()
        self.temp_dirs.append(temp_dir)
        metadata_dir = os.path.join(temp_dir.name, "metadata")
        targets_dir = os.path.join(temp_dir.name, "targets")
        os.mkdir(metadata_dir)
        os.mkdir(targets_dir)

        sim = RepositorySimulator()
        sim.add_target(
            "targets",
            b"app content",
            "modules/app",
        )
        sim.targets.targets["modules/app"].unrecognized_fields["custom"] = {
            "module": "app",
            "requires": [{"module": "lib", "interface": "lib-api"}],
        }
        sim.add_target("targets", b"lib content", "modules/lib")
        sim.targets.targets["modules/lib"].unrecognized_fields["custom"] = {
            "module": "lib",
            "provides": {"lib-api": "1.0.0"},
        }
        sim.targets.version += 1
        sim.update_snapshot()

        updater = Updater(
            metadata_dir,
            "https://example.com/metadata/",
            targets_dir,
            "https://example.com/targets/",
            sim,
            bootstrap=sim.signed_roots[0],
        )
        updater.refresh()
        return updater, sim

    def test_two_in_memory_mirrors_keep_download_state(self) -> None:
        first_updater, first_sim = self._mirror_updater()
        second_updater, second_sim = self._mirror_updater()

        first_before = first_updater.download_state()
        second_before = second_updater.download_state()
        fetches_before = (
            len(first_sim.fetch_tracker.metadata),
            len(first_sim.fetch_tracker.targets),
        )

        first_plan = first_updater.preflight_combination(
            ["modules/app"], {"lib-api": ">=1"}
        )
        second_plan = second_updater.preflight_combination(
            ["modules/app"], {"lib-api": ">=1"}
        )

        self.assertTrue(first_plan.ok)
        self.assertTrue(second_plan.ok)
        self.assertEqual(set(first_plan.targets), {"app", "lib"})
        self.assertEqual(set(second_plan.targets), {"app", "lib"})
        for name in first_plan.targets:
            self.assertEqual(
                first_plan.targets[name].hashes,
                second_plan.targets[name].hashes,
            )

        self.assertEqual(first_before, first_updater.download_state())
        self.assertEqual(second_before, second_updater.download_state())
        self.assertEqual(first_before, second_before)
        self.assertEqual(
            (
                len(first_sim.fetch_tracker.metadata),
                len(first_sim.fetch_tracker.targets),
            ),
            fetches_before,
        )
        self.assertEqual(first_sim.fetch_tracker.targets, [])
        self.assertEqual(second_sim.fetch_tracker.targets, [])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

Module declarations are read from a target's ``custom`` field. The following
shape is understood (all fields except ``module`` are optional)::

    "custom": {
        "module": "codec",
        "version": "1.4.2",
        "provides": {"codec-api": "2.1.0"},
        "requires": [
            {"module": "runtime", "interface": "runtime-api", "version": ">=2,<3"},
            "util"
        ],
        "config": "app-config/v3"
    }

``module`` defaults to the target path. ``requires`` may also be a list of
module names or a mapping of module names to version constraints. The caller
declaration is normally a mapping of interface names to versions or version
constraints; ``config_version`` declares the configuration generation the
caller can read.

The planner is deliberately side-effect free: it never refreshes metadata,
downloads a target, or writes to the Updater cache.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import re
from collections import abc
from collections.abc import Iterator
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any

from tuf.api.metadata import Metadata, TargetFile, Targets

            "order": self.order,
        }

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        return self.message


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ClientDownloadState:
    """Side-effect snapshot useful for asserting preflight is offline.

    The values are derived from already-loaded trusted metadata only.
    """

    roles: tuple[tuple[str, int, str], ...] = ()
    targets: tuple[str, ...] = ()

    def __eq__(self, other: object) -> bool:
        if not isinstance(other, ClientDownloadState):
            return NotImplemented
        return (self.roles, self.targets) == (other.roles, other.targets)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CombinationPlan:
    """Result of a combination preflight.

def _clause_tokens(expression: str) -> list[str]:
    normalized = re.sub(
        r"(~=|==|!=|>=|<=|>|<|=|\^|~)\s+", r"\1", expression.strip()
    )
    return [token for token in re.split(r"[,\s]+", normalized) if token]


def _build_alternative(expression: str) -> tuple[_Clause, ...] | None:
    text = expression.strip()
    if not text or text == "*":
        return None
    clauses: list[_Clause] = []
    for token in _clause_tokens(text):
        match = _CONSTRAINT_RE.match(token)
        if match is None:
            continue
        operator = match.group(1) or "=="
        version = match.group(2)
        bounds = _wildcard_bounds(version)
        if bounds is not None:
            lower, upper = bounds
    except (TypeError, ValueError):
        return str(value)


def _interface_mapping(value: Any) -> dict[str, str | None]:  # noqa: ANN401
    if not isinstance(value, abc.Mapping):
        return {}
    versions: dict[str, str | None] = {}
    for name, version in value.items():
        if not isinstance(name, str):
            continue
        if isinstance(version, abc.Mapping):
            version = version.get("version", version.get("constraint"))
        if version is None:
            versions[name] = None
        else:
            versions[name] = str(version)
    return versions


def _normalize_requirement(
    item: Any,  # noqa: ANN401
    source: str | None = None,
) -> DependencyRequirement | None:
    if isinstance(item, DependencyRequirement):
        return item
    if isinstance(item, str):
        return DependencyRequirement(module=item, source=source)
    if not isinstance(item, abc.Mapping):

        for item in value:
            requirement = _normalize_requirement(item, source)
            if requirement is not None:
                requirements.append(requirement)
    return requirements


def _extract_declaration(
    target: TargetFile,
    module: str | None = None,
    version: str | None = None,
    provides: abc.Mapping[str, str | None] | None = None,
    requires: Any = None,  # noqa: ANN401
    config: str | None = None,
    identity: str | None = None,
) -> ModuleDeclaration:
    custom = target.custom
    source = (
        custom
        if isinstance(custom, abc.Mapping)
        else target.unrecognized_fields
    )

    if module is None:
        for key in ("module", "module_name", "name", "package", "component"):
            value = source.get(key) if isinstance(source, abc.Mapping) else None
            if isinstance(value, str) and value:
                module = value
                break
    if module is None:
        module = target.path
    if version is None and isinstance(source, abc.Mapping):
        value = source.get("version", source.get("module_version"))
        version = str(value) if value is not None else None
    if config is None and isinstance(source, abc.Mapping):
        value = source.get(
            "config", source.get("configuration", source.get("config_version"))
        )
        config = _canonical_config(value) if value is not None else None
    if identity is None and isinstance(source, abc.Mapping):
        value = source.get("identity", source.get("id"))
        identity = str(value) if value is not None else None

    provided_map: dict[str, str | None] = {}
    if isinstance(source, abc.Mapping):
        for key in ("provides", "interfaces", "api"):
            provided_map.update(_interface_mapping(source.get(key)))
    if provides is not None:
        provided_map.update(provides)

    requirement_items: list[DependencyRequirement] = []
    if requires is not None:
        requirement_items.extend(_requirement_list(requires, module))
    if isinstance(source, abc.Mapping):
        for key in ("requires", "dependencies", "depends", "needs"):
            requirement_items.extend(_requirement_list(source.get(key), module))

    return ModuleDeclaration(
        module=module,
        version=version,
        provides=tuple(provided_map.items()),
        requires=tuple(requirement_items),
        config=config,
        identity=identity,
    )


def _parse_caller_declaration(
    declaration: Any,  # noqa: ANN401
) -> tuple[dict[str, str], str | None]:
    versions: dict[str, str] = {}
    config = None
    if declaration is None:
        return versions, config
    if isinstance(declaration, InterfaceDeclaration):
        return declaration.as_dict(), declaration.config
    if isinstance(declaration, abc.Mapping):
        for key in ("interfaces", "provides", "api", "versions"):
            nested = declaration.get(key)
            if isinstance(nested, abc.Mapping):
                versions.update(_interface_mapping(nested))
        for key in ("config", "configuration", "config_version"):
            if key in declaration:
                config = _canonical_config(declaration[key])
    def path_records(self, path: str) -> list[_Record]:
        return self.by_path.get(path, [])

    def module_records(self, module: str) -> list[_Record]:
        return self.by_module.get(module, [])


def _iter_view_entries(
    view: Any,  # noqa: ANN401
) -> Iterator[tuple[str | None, str, TargetFile]]:
    if view is None:
        return
    if hasattr(view, "iter_targets"):
        yield from view.iter_targets()
        return
    if hasattr(view, "_trusted_set"):
        yield from _iter_view_entries(view._trusted_set)
        return
    if isinstance(view, Targets):
        for path, target in view.targets.items():
            yield Targets.type, path, target
        return
    if isinstance(view, Metadata):
        if isinstance(view.signed, Targets):
            yield from _iter_view_entries(view.signed)
        return
    if isinstance(view, bytes):
        metadata = Metadata.from_bytes(view)
        if isinstance(metadata.signed, Targets):
            yield from _iter_view_entries(metadata.signed)
        return
    if isinstance(view, abc.Mapping):
        for role, value in view.items():
            if isinstance(value, TargetFile):
                yield str(role), value.path, value
            elif isinstance(value, Targets):
                for path, target in value.targets.items():
                    yield str(role), path, target
            elif isinstance(value, Metadata):
                if isinstance(value.signed, Targets):
                    for path, target in value.signed.targets.items():
                        yield str(role), path, target
            elif isinstance(value, bytes):
                metadata = Metadata.from_bytes(value)
                if isinstance(metadata.signed, Targets):
                    for path, target in metadata.signed.targets.items():
                        yield str(role), path, target
            elif isinstance(value, abc.Mapping):
                for path, target in value.items():
                    if isinstance(target, TargetFile):
                        yield str(role), str(path), target
            elif isinstance(value, abc.Iterable) and not isinstance(value, str):
                for item in value:
                    if isinstance(item, TargetFile):
                        yield str(role), item.path, item
            else:
                continue
        return
    if isinstance(view, abc.Iterable):
        for item in view:
            if isinstance(item, tuple) and len(item) == 3:
                role, path, target = item
                if isinstance(target, TargetFile):
                    yield str(role), str(path), target
            elif isinstance(item, TargetFile):
                yield None, item.path, item
            elif isinstance(item, (Targets, Metadata, bytes)):
                yield from _iter_view_entries(item)


def _build_view(view: Any) -> _View:  # noqa: ANN401
    result = _View()
    order = 0
    for role, path, target in _iter_view_entries(view):
        declaration = _extract_declaration(target)
        result.add(
            _Record(
                module=declaration.module,
                target=target,
                channel=role,
                declaration=declaration,
                order=order,
            )
        )
        order += 1
    return result

def _target_from_value(
    value: Any,
    view: _View,  # noqa: ANN401
) -> tuple[TargetFile | None, str | None]:
    if isinstance(value, TargetFile):
        return value, None
    if isinstance(value, str):
        records = view.path_records(value)
        if not records:
            return None, None
        return records[0].target, records[0].channel
    if isinstance(value, abc.Mapping):
        for key in ("target", "targetfile", "file"):
            candidate = value.get(key)
            if isinstance(candidate, TargetFile):
                return candidate, None
            if isinstance(candidate, str):
                return _target_from_value(candidate, view)
        for key in ("path", "target_path"):
            candidate = value.get(key)
            if isinstance(candidate, str):
                return _target_from_value(candidate, view)
    return None, None

def _resolve_closure(
    candidates: list[_Record],
    view: _View,
    max_depth: int,
) -> _Resolution:
    failures: list[_Failure] = []
    selected: dict[str, _Record] = {}
    selected_order: dict[str, int] = {}
    module_records: dict[str, list[_Record]] = {}

    for record in candidates:
        module_records.setdefault(record.module, []).append(record)
    for module, records in module_records.items():
        conflict = _identity_conflict(
            module, _unique_records(records), records[0].order
        )
        if conflict is not None:
            failures.append(_Failure(records[0].order, conflict))
            continue
        selected[module] = records[0]
        selected_order[module] = records[0].order

    queue = list(selected)
    edges: list[Dependency] = []
    edge_order = max((record.order for record in candidates), default=0) + 1
    expanded: set[str] = set()

    while queue:
        module = queue.pop(0)
        if module in expanded:
            continue
        expanded.add(module)
        record = selected[module]
        for requirement in record.declaration.requires:
            order = edge_order
            edge_order += 1
            dependency, failure = _resolve_dependency(
                requirement, selected, module_records, view, order
            )
            if failure is not None:
                failures.append(failure)
                continue
            assert dependency is not None
            provided_version = None
            if requirement.interface is not None:
                provided_version = dependency.declaration.provided_version(
                    requirement.interface
    return _Resolution(selected, edges, failures, selected_order)


def _check_cycles(
    selected: dict[str, _Record],
    edges: list[Dependency],
    selected_order: dict[str, int],
    failures: list[_Failure],
) -> None:
    adjacency: dict[str, list[Dependency]] = {}
    for edge in edges:
        adjacency.setdefault(edge.module, []).append(edge)
    state: dict[str, int] = {}
    stack: list[str] = []

    def visit(module: str) -> None:
        state[module] = 1
        stack.append(module)
        for edge in adjacency.get(module, []):
            dependency = edge.dependency
            if dependency not in selected:
                continue
            if state.get(dependency) == 1:
                start = stack.index(dependency)
                cycle = tuple(stack[start:] + [dependency])
                failures.append(
                    _Failure(
                        edge.order,
                        CombinationConflict(
                            kind="cycle",
                            message=("Dependency cycle: " + " -> ".join(cycle)),
                            group=tuple(
                                f"{left} -> {right}"
                                for left, right in zip(cycle, cycle[1:])
                            ),
                            modules=cycle,
                            cycle=cycle,
                            reason="mutual-dependency",
                            order=edge.order,
                        ),
                    )
                )
            elif state.get(dependency, 0) == 0:
            f"{self.source} requires {target} {self.constraint} "
            f"(provided {self.actual_version})"
        )

        record = resolution.records[edge.dependency]
        interface = edge.interface

    __call__ = resolve

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class _Clause:
    operator: str
    version: str
    version_key: tuple[tuple[int, ...], str] | None

    def matches(self, version_key: tuple[tuple[int, ...], str] | None) -> bool:
        if version_key is None:
            return self.operator in {"==", "="}
        other = self.version_key
        if other is None:
            return False
        left_numbers, left_suffix = version_key
        right_numbers, right_suffix = other
        width = max(len(left_numbers), len(right_numbers))
        padded_left = left_numbers + (0,) * (width - len(left_numbers))
        padded_right = right_numbers + (0,) * (width - len(right_numbers))
        if self.operator in {"==", "="}:
            return left_numbers == right_numbers and left_suffix == right_suffix
        if self.operator == "!=":
            return not (
                left_numbers == right_numbers and left_suffix == right_suffix
            )
        if padded_left == padded_right and left_suffix != right_suffix:
            # A prerelease sorts before the corresponding release.
            comparison = -1 if left_suffix else 1
        elif padded_left != padded_right:
            comparison = -1 if padded_left < padded_right else 1
        elif left_suffix == right_suffix:
            comparison = 0
        elif not left_suffix:
            comparison = 1
        elif not right_suffix:
            comparison = -1
        else:
            comparison = -1 if left_suffix < right_suffix else 1
        return {
            ">": comparison > 0,
            ">=": comparison >= 0,
            "<": comparison < 0,
            alternatives.append(_build_alternative(alternative))
        self._alternatives = tuple(alternatives)
        parsed = _parse_version(self.expression)
        self.exact = (
            self.expression
            if parsed is not None
            and not re.search(r"[<>=!~^*xX]", self.expression)
            else None
        )

    @property
    def any(self) -> bool:
        """Return whether this constraint accepts every version."""
        return all(clauses is None for clauses in self._alternatives)

    def matches(self, version: str | None) -> bool:
        """Return whether ``version`` satisfies this constraint."""
        if version is None:
            return self.any
        version_key = _parse_version(version)
        for clauses in self._alternatives:
            if clauses is None:
                return True
            if all(clause.matches(version_key) for clause in clauses):
                return True
        return False

    def intersects(self, other: VersionConstraint) -> bool:
        """Return whether the two constraints have a common version."""
        if self.any or other.any:
            return True
        if self.exact is not None:
            return other.matches(self.exact)
        if other.exact is not None:
            return self.matches(other.exact)
        samples = self._samples() | other._samples()
        return any(
            self.matches(sample) and other.matches(sample) for sample in samples
        )

    def _samples(self) -> set[str]:
        samples: set[str] = set()
        for clauses in self._alternatives:
            if clauses is None:
                continue
            for clause in clauses:
                if clause.version_key is None:
                    samples.add(clause.version)
                    continue
                samples.add(clause.version)
                numbers = list(clause.version_key[0])
                for index, number in enumerate(numbers):
                    for delta in (-1, 0, 1):
                        if number + delta < 0:
                            continue
                        candidate = list(numbers)
        return versions, config
    if isinstance(declaration, InterfaceDeclaration):
        return declaration.as_dict(), declaration.config
    if isinstance(declaration, abc.Mapping):
        for key in ("interfaces", "provides", "api", "versions"):
            nested = declaration.get(key)
            if isinstance(nested, abc.Mapping):
                versions.update(_interface_mapping(nested))
        for key in ("config", "configuration", "config_version"):
            if key in declaration:
                config = _canonical_config(declaration[key])
                break
        reserved = {
            "interfaces",
            "provides",
            "api",
            "versions",
            "config",
            "configuration",
            "config_version",
        }
        for name, version in _interface_mapping(declaration).items():
            if name in reserved:
                continue
            versions[name] = version or "*"
        return versions, config
    if isinstance(declaration, abc.Iterable):
        for item in declaration:
            if isinstance(item, (tuple, list)) and len(item) == 2:
                versions[str(item[0])] = str(item[1])
            elif isinstance(item, DependencyRequirement) and item.interface:
                versions[item.interface] = item.version or "*"
        return versions, config
    for key in ("versions", "interfaces", "provides", "api"):
        value = getattr(declaration, key, None)
        if value is not None:
            versions.update(_interface_mapping(value))
    config_value = getattr(declaration, "config", None)
    if config_value is not None:
        config = _canonical_config(config_value)
    return versions, config

# Changelog

## Unreleased

### Changed

* Add Python 3.15 to tested versions, remove 3.10 from tested versions.
  Require at least 3.11

## v7.0.1

### Fixed

* Fix hash method on Role and DelegatedRole (#2973)
* Fix incorrect dependency range for urllib3 (#2990)
* ngclient: Fix connection exception handling (#2964, #2993)
* ngclient: Fix incorrect delegation in diamond-shape case (#2978)
* ngclient: Avoid use of symlink on Windows: it requires privileges (#2981)

## v7.0.0
ngclient
========

The ngclient module contains a complete TUF client library implementation.

* :doc:`tuf.ngclient.updater` implements the `detailed client workflow`_
* :doc:`tuf.ngclient.config` provides optional configuration for the updater
* :doc:`tuf.ngclient.fetcher` can be used for optional low-level network I/O control


.. toctree::
   :hidden:

   tuf.ngclient.updater
   tuf.ngclient.config
   tuf.ngclient.fetcher

.. _detailed client workflow: https://theupdateframework.github.io/specification/latest/#detailed-client-workflow

## Next-gen TUF client for Python

This package provides modules for TUF client implementers.

**tuf.ngclient.Updater** is a class that implements the client workflow
described in the TUF specification (see
https://theupdateframework.github.io/specification/latest/#detailed-client-workflow)

**tuf.ngclient.FetcherInterface** is an abstract class that client
implementers can implement a concrete class of in order to reuse their
own networking/download libraries -- a Requests-based implementation is
used by default.

This package:
* Aims to be a clean, easy-to-validate reference client implementation
  written in modern Python
* At the same time aims to be the library of choice for anyone
  implementing a TUF client in Python: light-weight, easy to integrate
  and with minimal required dependencies
* Is still under development but is planned to become the default client
  in this implementation (i.e., the older `tuf.client` will be deprecated
  in the future)

ngclient
========

The ngclient module contains a complete TUF client library implementation.

* :doc:`tuf.ngclient.updater` implements the `detailed client workflow`_
* :doc:`tuf.ngclient.config` provides optional configuration for the updater
* :doc:`tuf.ngclient.fetcher` can be used for optional low-level network I/O control
* :doc:`tuf.ngclient.combination` provides the offline target-combination preflight


.. toctree::
   :hidden:

   tuf.ngclient.updater

   tuf.ngclient.combination

.. _detailed client workflow: https://theupdateframework.github.io/specification/latest/#detailed-client-workflow

## Next-gen TUF client for Python

This package provides modules for TUF client implementers.

**tuf.ngclient.Updater** is a class that implements the client workflow
described in the TUF specification (see
https://theupdateframework.github.io/specification/latest/#detailed-client-workflow)

**tuf.ngclient.CombinationPreflight** resolves the transitive closure of a
candidate set of verified targets and checks interface/config compatibility
offline, before the application starts.

**tuf.ngclient.FetcherInterface** is an abstract class that client
implementers can implement a concrete class of in order to reuse their
own networking/download libraries -- a Requests-based implementation is
used by default.

This package:
* Aims to be a clean, easy-to-validate reference client implementation
  written in modern Python
* At the same time aims to be the library of choice for anyone
  implementing a TUF client in Python: light-weight, easy to integrate
  and with minimal required dependencies
* Is still under development but is planned to become the default client
  in this implementation (i.e., the older `tuf.client` will be deprecated

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class _ConstraintEntry:
    order: int
    module: str
    interface: str | None
    constraint: VersionConstraint | None
    actual_version: str | None
    source: str

    def description(self) -> str:
        if self.interface is None:
            target = self.module
        else:
            target = f"{self.module}::{self.interface}"
        if self.constraint is None:
            return f"{self.source} requires {target}"
        if self.actual_version is None:
            return f"{self.source} requires {target} {self.constraint}"
        return (
            f"{self.source} requires {target} {self.constraint} "
            f"(provided {self.actual_version})"
        )


def _check_requirements(  # noqa: C901, PLR0912

    caller_config: str | None,
) -> list[_Failure]:
    failures: list[_Failure] = []

        failing = False
        for entry in group:
            if entry.constraint is None:

            if entry.actual_version is None:

+    def iter_targets(self) -> Iterator[tuple[str, str, TargetFile]]:
+        """Yield ``(role, path, target)`` for trusted targets metadata.
+
+        Iteration follows the order in which roles were added to the trusted
+        set. Only data that has already passed TUF verification is returned;
+        this method never performs network or filesystem I/O.
+        """
+        for role, signed in self._trusted_set.items():
+            if isinstance(signed, Targets):
+                for path, target in signed.targets.items():
+                    yield role, path, target
+
+    def iter_roles(self) -> Iterator[tuple[str, Signed]]:
+        """Yield ``(role, signed)`` pairs in trusted-set insertion order."""
+        yield from self._trusted_set.items()
+

+    def preflight_combination(
+        self,
+        candidate_targets: object = None,
+        interface_versions: object = None,
+        **kwargs: object,
+    ) -> CombinationPlan:
+        """Run the offline combination preflight on trusted metadata.
+
+        The preflight only inspects metadata already present in this
+        ``Updater``'s verified view. It never refreshes metadata and never
+        downloads a target: candidates that are not already part of the
+        verified view are reported as missing instead.
+
+        Args:
+            candidate_targets: Target paths, ``TargetFile`` objects, or
+                channel-to-target mappings. See
+                :func:`tuf.ngclient.combination.preflight_combination`.
+            interface_versions: Caller's interface-version declaration.
+            **kwargs: Additional options for the preflight, for example
+                ``config_version`` or ``allow_cycles``.
+
+        Returns:
+            A ``CombinationPlan`` containing either the adoptable closure and
+            dependency evidence or the earliest conflict.
+        """
+        candidate_targets = kwargs.pop("candidates", candidate_targets)
+        interface_versions = kwargs.pop("interfaces", interface_versions)
+        return preflight_combination(
+            self._trusted_set,
+            candidate_targets,
+            interface_versions,
+            **kwargs,
+        )
+
+    def preflight_target_combination(
+        self,
+        candidate_targets: object,
+        interface_versions: object = None,

+    ) -> CombinationPlan:

+        return self.preflight_combination(

+    def download_state(self) -> ClientDownloadState:
+        """Return a read-only snapshot of the client's verified state.
+
+        The snapshot covers metadata roles/versions and the target identities
+        currently present in the trusted view. It can be used to assert that
+        an offline preflight did not mutate download state.
+        """
+        roles = tuple(
+            sorted(
+                (role, signed.version, signed.type)
+                for role, signed in self._trusted_set.iter_roles()
+            )
+        )
+        targets = tuple(
+            sorted(
+                f"{role}:{path}:{target.length}:"
+                + repr(sorted(target.hashes.items()))
+                for role, path, target in self._trusted_set.iter_targets()
+            )
+        )
+        return ClientDownloadState(roles, targets)

    def resolve(
        self,
        candidates: Any = _UNSET,  # noqa: ANN401
        interfaces: Any = _UNSET,  # noqa: ANN401
        *,
        config_version: Any = _UNSET,  # noqa: ANN401
        config: Any = _UNSET,  # noqa: ANN401
        caller_config: Any = _UNSET,  # noqa: ANN401
        allow_cycles: bool | None = None,
        max_depth: int | None = None,
        raise_on_conflict: bool = False,
    ) -> CombinationPlan:
        """Resolve the closure and return a plan or the first conflict."""
        selected_candidates = (
            self.candidates if candidates is _UNSET else candidates
        )
        selected_interfaces = (
            self.interfaces if interfaces is _UNSET else interfaces
        )
        selected_config = (
            self.config_version if config_version is _UNSET else config_version
        )
        cycles = self.allow_cycles if allow_cycles is None else allow_cycles
        depth = self.max_depth if max_depth is None else max_depth
        if depth <= 0:
            raise ValueError("max_depth must be positive")

        view = _build_view(self.metadata)
        candidate_records, candidate_failures = _candidate_records(
            selected_candidates, view, strict_view=self.metadata is not None
        )
        caller_versions, caller_config = _parse_caller_declaration(
            selected_interfaces
        )
        if selected_config is not None:
            caller_config = _canonical_config(selected_config)

        resolution = _resolve_closure(
            candidate_records, view, depth, caller_versions
        )
        failures = [*candidate_failures, *resolution.failures]
        failures.extend(
            _check_requirements(resolution, caller_versions, caller_config)
        )
        if cycles:
            failures = [
                failure

===OUTPUT call_00_vxytbkyxd6xbv8ihm52tx49r ===
Exit code: 0
Wall time: 0 seconds
Output:
    config_version: Any = None,  # noqa: ANN401
    config: Any = None,  # noqa: ANN401
    caller_config: Any = None,  # noqa: ANN401
    allow_cycles: bool = False,
    max_depth: int = 256,
    raise_on_conflict: bool = False,
    **kwargs: Any,  # noqa: ANN401
) -> CombinationPlan:
    """Compute the offline adoptable closure for candidate targets.

    This is the functional entry point of the module. The first three
    positional arguments are the canonical form; common keyword aliases are
    accepted for integration convenience.

    Args:
        metadata: Already verified metadata view.
        candidates: Candidate targets.
        interfaces: Caller's interface-version declaration.
        config_version: Optional caller configuration generation.
        allow_cycles: Keep cycles in the closure instead of reporting them.
        max_depth: Maximum module count in the closure.
        raise_on_conflict: Raise ``CombinationConflictError`` on failure.

    Returns:
        A ``CombinationPlan`` with either adoptable targets and dependency
        evidence, or the first conflict.
    """
    view_value = _first_value(
        metadata,
        verified_metadata,
        metadata_view,
        trusted_set,
        view,
    )
    candidate_value = _first_value(
        candidates, candidate_targets, targets, kwargs.pop("candidate", None)
    )
    interface_value = _first_value(
        interfaces,
        caller_interfaces,
        interface_versions,
        interface_declaration,
        kwargs.pop("declarations", None),
    )
    if kwargs:
        unknown = ", ".join(sorted(kwargs))
        raise TypeError(f"Unexpected keyword argument(s): {unknown}")
    preflight = CombinationPreflight(
        view_value,
        candidate_value,
        interface_value,
        allow_cycles=allow_cycles,
        max_depth=max_depth,
        config_version=_first_value(config_version, config, caller_config),
    )
    return preflight.resolve(raise_on_conflict=raise_on_conflict)


def resolve_combination(*args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> CombinationPlan:  # noqa: ANN401
    """Alias for :func:`preflight_combination`."""
    return preflight_combination(*args, **kwargs)

        depth = self.max_depth if max_depth is None else max_depth
        if depth <= 0:
            raise ValueError("max_depth must be positive")

        view = _build_view(self.metadata)
        candidate_records, candidate_failures = _candidate_records(
            selected_candidates, view, strict_view=self.metadata is not None
        )
        caller_versions, caller_config = _parse_caller_declaration(
            selected_interfaces
        )
        if selected_config is not None:
            caller_config = _canonical_config(selected_config)

        resolution = _resolve_closure(
            candidate_records, view, depth, caller_versions
        )
        failures = [*candidate_failures, *resolution.failures]
        failures.extend(
            _check_requirements(resolution, caller_versions, caller_config)
        )
        if cycles:
            failures = [
                failure
                for failure in failures
                if failure.conflict.kind != "cycle"
            ]
        conflict = _find_best_failure(failures)
        if conflict is not None:
            plan = _empty_plan(
                conflict,
                resolution.edges,
                resolution.records,
                caller_versions,
                caller_config,
            )
        else:
            plan = CombinationPlan(
                targets={
                    module: record.target
                    for module, record in resolution.records.items()
                },
                dependencies=tuple(resolution.edges),
                caller_interfaces=tuple(caller_versions.items()),
                caller_config=caller_config,
                attempted_targets={
                    module: record.target
                    for module, record in resolution.records.items()
                },
            )
        if raise_on_conflict:
            plan.raise_for_conflict()
        return plan

    allow_cycles: bool = False,
    max_depth: int = 256,
    raise_on_conflict: bool = False,
    **kwargs: Any,  # noqa: ANN401
) -> CombinationPlan:
    """Compute the offline adoptable closure for candidate targets.

    This is the functional entry point of the module. The first three
    positional arguments are the canonical form; common keyword aliases are
    accepted for integration convenience.

    Args:
        metadata: Already verified metadata view.
        candidates: Candidate targets.
        interfaces: Caller's interface-version declaration.
        config_version: Optional caller configuration generation.
        allow_cycles: Keep cycles in the closure instead of reporting them.
